ADNOC Classification: Internal
HSE Management System
HSE Governance & Administration Standards
Incident Notification, Investigation & Reporting
1. INTRODUCTION
ADNOC places utmost care on prevention of incidents. ADNOC, has established mandatory
requirements for ensuring timely notification, investigation and reporting of all HSE incidents with an
objective of learning from incidents and avoiding recurrence. ADNOC requires that each Group
Company shall record, manage and report all incidents regardless of the incident location or cause.
Reporting is to reflect work-related injuries/illnesses incidents affecting both Employees and
Reportable Contractors (and in the case of fatalities, affected Third Parties), whether these occur on
Company premises or in other work environments / situations.
The presence or absence of fault or preventability is not relevant in determining whether or not
an injury or illness is recordable.
The objective of this Standard is to ensure that ADNOC Group and Contractors are supplied with a
standardized process to record, notify, investigate and report HSE incidents including non-accidental
deaths (NAD) within a stipulated timeframe.
ADNOC Incident notification and reporting requirements give rise to the question "is every case
resulting from an event or exposure on the employer's premises / work environment considered work-
related?" The answer is that each case begins with the presumption that all cases occurring in the work
environment are work-related, unless demonstrated to be otherwise based on detailed investigation.
The nature of the activity in which the worker is involved, the degree of employer control over the
worker's activity, the preventability of the event, and the concept of fault do not affect this presumption.
This approach unifies incident recording, reporting, investigation, learning and continuous improvement
processes and provides opportunities for consistent trend analyses across ADNOC Group and
Contractors.
The key elements of notification and reporting of these high severity incidents to ADNOC specifically
include:
Setting the time limits for notification / reporting of such incidents;
Providing the details for each reportable incident to gauge the initial response and mitigation
arrangements;
Conducting an appropriate and adequate investigation to identify the vulnerable areas and gaps in
the management systems;
Ensuring continual improvement through implementation of action plans for recommendations and
closure of actions arising out of the investigations; and
Learning from Incidents and information sharing to avoid recurrence of similar incidents in future.
The Standard addresses requirements for establishing incidents’ work relatedness explained through
various scenarios such as commuting, travel, company’s parking lots, remote locations, off employer’s
premises and exceptions to work relatedness.
The Standard elaborates “Hurt Free Approach” based reporting developed to drive cultural change as an
alternative to the traditional Treatment-based Approach. Hurt free reporting is intended to enable the
persistent pursuit of an injury and illness free workplace aligned with ADNOC core values to help promote
the desired culture of 100% HSE. It provides a framework that enables a culture of caring while also
allowing for insightful trends analysis via consistent actual severity assessment and an integral potential
consequence assessment. The Hurt Free Approach looks at both the actual injury and illness
consequence that resulted from an event as well as the potential consequence that might have occurred
if circumstances had been slightly different.
The Standard establishes reporting requirements for incidents involving Suppliers, Third Parties and
various types of Contractors. It stipulates criteria for classification of Reportable & Excluded Contractors.
In addition to the immediate notification and reporting requirements as stipulated in this Standard, at
the end of each year ADNOC requires that all Group Companies report statistics for all incidents as a
part of the HSE Performance Report in compliance with the requirements of ADNOC HSE Performance
Monitoring & Reporting Standard [HSE-GA-ST08].
Standard No.: HSE-GA-ST04 Version No.: 1 Effective date: Dec., 2019 Page 7 of 184

ADNOC Classification: Internal
HSE Management System
HSE Governance & Administration Standards
Incident Notification, Investigation & Reporting
2. PURPOSE
This Standard establishes the requirements and defines framework for the complete lifecycle of
incidents within the ADNOC Group from recording, reporting through investigation, development of
action plan, implementation, follow-up and learning from incidents.
It stipulates the mandatory requirements for incident notification, recording, classification, investigation,
reporting, analysis and sharing of lessons learned within the ADNOC Group for all incidents including
Occupational Safety & Health, Process Safety, Environment, Welfare, Reputation (such as workers
unrest, strikes, violence), others, etc. The Standard also outlines requirements for notification,
investigation and reporting Non-Accidental Death (NAD).
This Standard establishes criteria for determining the work relatedness and defines process and roles
of the various parties within ADNOC Group who have responsibilities for incident recording, reporting,
investigation and follow-up, to ensure that consistent, timely and effective incident reporting and
investigation requirements are implemented.
The document also highlights the assurance processes involving ADNOC and Group Companies to
ensure effective learning from incidents and avoiding recurrence of any such incidents across the
ADNOC Group.
3. SCOPE
This Standard is applicable to all ADNOC Group (Directorates & Functions at Head Quarter, Group
Companies and Affiliates) and Contractors.
The notification and reporting requirements stipulated in this Standard are for incidents and
occupational injuries/illnesses affecting directly ADNOC Group employees and reportable contractors
as well as third party fatalities (Refer to Section 7.8.1). This includes other ADNOC entities such as
ADNOC Technical Academy, ADNOC Clinics, ADNOC Schools, etc. Incidents occurring in a joint
venture under a Group Company’s operating control are also included.
ADNOC Affiliates for example ADOC, BUNDOQ, TOTAL ABK are also subject to comply with
requirements of this standards as per directions of the Supreme Petroleum Council [refer to ADNOC
HSE Governance Framework Standard [HSE-GA-ST01].
For ADNOC Joint Ventures, Share Holders, Projects and Concession Agreement Holders within the
UAE the requirements of this Standard are equally binding.
For other ADNOC Interests and Businesses outside the UAE, the relevant ADNOC legal documents
for example Memorandum of Understanding (MoU), Contractual Arrangements, etc. as governed by
ADNOC Legal, Governance & Compliance shall constitute compliance with ADNOC HSE Standards.
As a minimum, such business entities shall report and investigate high severity incidents and share
investigation reports with ADNOC Group HSE function. Upon receiving reports, ADNOC may ask for
additional action / information as deemed necessary. Reported incidents will be reviewed for ADNOC
Group HSE Performance KPIs and statistics as applicable [refer to ADNOC HSE Performance
Monitoring & Reporting Standard HSE-GA-ST08].
ADNOC HSE Governance Framework Standard [HSE-GA-ST01] stipulates compliance obligations
applicable to various types of ADNOC Business and Operations.

4. LAWS AND REGULATIONS
UAE Legislations applicable to this Standard includes but not limited to:
(i) Law No. (1) of 1988 Establishing the Supreme Petroleum Council (SPC)
(ii) Federal Law No. 8 (1980) UAE Labour Law and its Amendments Chapter V: Safety, Protection,
and the Health and Social Care of the Employees; Article (91).
(iii) Federal Law No. 24 (1999): Protection and Development of the Environment.
(iv) Abu Dhabi Law No. 12 of 1973: Regulating the Petroleum Ports
(v) Law No. 17 (2009) concerning the protection of safety areas around petroleum fixtures,
installations and artificial islands in the marine regions in the Emirate of Abu Dhabi
(vi) UAE Ministerial Order No. (32), 1982. Specifying Preventive Methods and Measures for
Protecting Workers against Work Hazards.
(vii) UAE Ministerial Order No. (37/2), 1982. Establishing the level of medical attention the employer
is obliged to provide to his workers.
(viii) Federal Authority for Nuclear Regulations FANR- REG-24 Version 1 Basic Safety Standards for
Facilities and Activities involving Ionizing Radiation other than in Nuclear Facilities
(ix) Health Authority Abu Dhabi (HAAD) Standard for Notifying Infectious Diseases in the Emirates
of Abu Dhabi of 2010 [PHP/PHR/S/02]
ADNOC Group and Contractors shall ensure that their activities comply with all relevant Federal and
Local laws and regulations at all times including any that may be introduced after the publication of this
Standard.

5. DEFINITIONS & ABBREVIATIONS
For the purposes of this Standard the following definitions will be used.
TERMS DESCRIPTION
ADNOC Abu Dhabi National Oil Company
ADNOC Group ADNOC Group includes the Directorates & Functions in ADNOC Head Quarter,
Group Companies and Affiliates
Affiliated Company or Those Abu Dhabi based companies engaged in the petroleum or petroleum
Affiliate services business with direct line accountability to the Supreme Petroleum
Council. For the purpose of ADNOC HSE Standards, where the term ‘Group
Companies’ is used this includes the “Affiliated Companies”.
Aircraft Aircrafts as referred in this Standard includes but not limited to fixed wing (jet,
turboprop, piston), helicopter (turbine, piston), seaplanes, etc. Refer to ADNOC
Air Transport Operations Safety Standard (HSE-OS-ST26) for details on aircraft.
Amputation An amputation is the traumatic loss of a limb or other external body part.
Amputations include a part, such as a limb or appendage that has been severed,
cut off, amputated (either completely or partially); fingertip amputations with or
without bone loss; medical amputations resulting from irreparable damage;
amputations of body parts that have since been reattached. Amputations do not
include avulsions, enucleations, degloving, scalping, severed ears, or broken or
chipped teeth.
All work related amputation cases shall be recorded and notified as Permanent
Partial Disability (PPD) within 24 hours of the incident occurrence. Any
subsequent change in injury classification shall be subject to supporting medical
documents including No Disability Assessment Certificate (as applicable) issued
from the treating hospital licensed by relevant Federal or Local Govt. Authority.
Asset Damage Asset / Property damage is a direct loss of, or damage to, plant, equipment, tools
or materials resulting from an incident. The consequences of the incident may
include both direct loss or damage or consequential business loss. The
consequences should be assessed on the ADNOC Risk Matrix (Appendix 1)
Bareboat Charter A shipping agreement wherein administrative and technical assistance is not
provided by the vessel owner. The charterer is responsible for providing crew,
operating expenses, insurance and port expenses. In addition, the charterer is
given control and possession with legal and financial responsibility during the
agreement period.
Brownfield Project Brownfield project refers to project of expansion, extension, addition or
revamping of units, equipment, utilities or services on an existing facility
Commuting Commuting referred in this Standard includes
 Travel between the employee's home and a regular place of work
 Travel from home to first work site and travel from last work site to home.
 Travel between a workers’ identified work location and any location for
personal business, including a restaurant.
 Travel between workers’ established ‘controlled housing’ site to the first
worksite or to any location for personal business, including a restaurant.
 Travel between home and a non-company event e.g. local conference or
other similar
Company Premises See Employer’s Premises

Incident Notification, Investigation & Reporting
TERMS DESCRIPTION
Contractor A ‘Contractor’ is defined as an individual or organization having a contract / sub-
contract with ADNOC Group for the provision of services, equipment or
performance of works. The term “Contractor” as used in the ADNOC Group
Contracts Procedure is synonymous with the terms including but not limited to
“Consultant”, “Sub-Contractor”, “Service Provider” or “Supplier.
See also definition of Excluded Contractors.
Contractual Mode 1 ADNOC Leads & Directs Work. HSE Performance Monitored & Reported to ADNOC.
ADNOC HSEMS applies. The contractor provides people, processes and tools for
the execution of the contract under the supervision, instructions and HSEMS of
ADNOC. The contractor has a management system to provide assurance that the
personnel for whom it is responsible are qualified and fit for the work and that the
processes, tools, materials and equipment they provide are properly maintained and
suitable. Typically, this applies to scopes of minor modification or maintenance/
turnaround work on ADNOC owned or operated site.
Contractual Mode 2 Contractor Leads & Directs Work. HSE Performance Monitored & Reported to
ADNOC. Contractor HSEMS applies. ADNOC reviews and approves the
Contractor’s HSEMS ensuring that it is aligned with ADNOC HSEMS.
The contractor executes all aspects of the contract under its own HSEMS,
providing the necessary supervision and verifying the proper functioning of its
HSEMS. ADNOC verifies and assures as per agreed contract terms and
condition the overall effectiveness of the HSE management controls put in place
by the contractor, including its interface with subcontractors.
This could apply to scopes of work on sites owned/operated either by ADNOC,
contractor, or by other persons having no relationship with company or
contractors. The work can be fenced off on a separate site, like a drilling location
or separate project site. This also applies to ADNOC Turnkey Projects.
Contractual Mode 3 The contractor provides people, process, equipment, and/or facilities for the
execution of the contract under its own supervision, instruction, and HSEMS. It
requires no interfacing or bridging with the ADNOC HSEMS and contractor is not
required to report HSE performance data including incidents to ADNOC.
This could apply to scopes of work on sites owned/ operated by contractor or by
other persons having no relationship with company or contractors, and include
examples such as: manufacturing of products produced for the open market,
which client purchases (e.g. plant equipment) or manufacturing of components in
a factory together with the manufacture of components for other customers.
Contractor Employee A person employed by a Contractor or Contractor’s Subcontractor(s) who is
directly involved in execution of prescribed work under a Mode 1 or Mode 2
contract with the ADNOC Group.
Controlled Housing Housing sites that are owned, managed, or controlled by the Company (or by the
Reportable Contractor for camps/accommodations set up solely for the benefit of
the Company) and furnished to Workers as a condition of employment (e.g., living
accommodations on off-shore platforms, camps in some remote areas, housing
in urban areas as applicable).
Executive Leadership ADNOC Executive Leadership Team comprising of All CEOs and Directors.
Team [ELT]
Employer’s Premises Employer’s Premises (also known as Company Premises) consist of the total
establishment. They include the primary work locations and other areas that are
considered part of the employer’s general work area. The work locations include
employer’s owned, leased or rented areas, rights of ways etc.

TERMS DESCRIPTION
Employee "Employee" means an employee hired directly under the sponsorship of ADNOC
or a Group Company including [Special Contract Employees], [Secondees
In/Out] and [Manpower Agency Contract Employees], but excluding [Industrial
Shareholder Secondees] and [Service Contract Personnel].
"Industrial Shareholders Secondees" means secondees from third party
Shareholders of a Group Company
"Secondee In/Out” means an Employee seconded in or out between ADNOC
and a Group Company for a specific period of time.
"Service Contract Employee" means a person that is employed and sponsored
by a third party to work for an ADNOC Group company.
"Special Contract Personnel" means a person who is employed by the ADNOC
Group and offered special terms and conditions of employment. A Special
Contract Personnel’s contract may be limited or unlimited.
Employment Employment means all work or activity performed in carrying out an assignment
or request of an ADNOC Group member company or Contractor, including
related activities not specifically covered by the assignment or request.
Employment also includes activities undertaken at the company’s request or for
company business reasons, outside of normal working hours.
Event An unplanned or uncontrolled outcome of a business operation or activity that
has or could have contributed to an injury or physical damage or environmental
damage.
Excluded Contractors ADNOC has determined, the following types of Contractors do not meet the
definition of “Reportable Contractor” due to the lack of client control over their
activities. Given the lack of Company control of their activities, these Contractors
are not considered to be Reportable Contractors.
Exception: Excluded Contractors (as mentioned below) while performing work
activity under client’s controls such as PTW, Supervision, Adminstrative Controls
as per Contractual Mode 1 or 2 shall be Reportable. Refer to definitions of
Contractual Mode 1 & 2.
 Office equipment service personnel (such as copier repair, telephone or
audio-visual equipment workers) where the equipment is purchased or
leased by the Company, and repairs are provided by the equipment supplier
as part of or incidental to the purchase or lease agreement (e.g.,
maintenance agreement or warranty).
 Vending personnel, who stock food / snack vending machines on Company
Premises.
 Delivery personnel at convenience stores, who may also provide stocking
services (e.g., of bread, beverages) as part of the standard delivery process.
 The Contractors that may perform work on Company Premises and that
meet all of the following four criteria:
a) No Company control (work direction, oversight) over activities is
provided or required.
b) No Company escort (unless for security purposes only) is provided or
required.
c) No service or work is provided by the driver / crew beyond the delivery
or pick-up of materials (e.g., no assembly of materials or equipment,
no equipment or materials stocking)
d) No physical connection to the process / operation / well site is
required.

ADNOC Classification: Internal

HSE Governance & Administration Standards

TERMS DESCRIPTION
These include:
- Standard Services: Providers of community-type services (e.g.,
municipal trash disposal service) or a widely available general service
(commercial trash hauler) where the Company (or its reportable
contractor or sub-contractor) does not provide oversight or direction.
This exception however does not apply to Collection or handling of
process waste or other services directly related to operations.
- Simple Delivery Services – Delivery service provider that has a
commercial relationship with the Company, Contractor or sub-contractor;
but whose drivers and crews provide no services or work beyond the
delivery or pick-up of materials.
- Mail, courier, service, and incidental delivery services on ADNOC
premises that are not contractually dedicated for exclusive use by
ADNOC engaging with other firms as a normal activity (e.g. office supply,
vending machine deliveries).
- Note: Simple delivery can include the act of loading/unloading (e.g., of a
pallet from truck using a forklift or other equipment).
Exclusion Zone
A 500 meter radius surrounding Company offshore facilities (including platforms,
offshore terminals and the vertical projection of subsea components and subsea
pipelines). This is analogous to a fence line around an onshore facility. The 500
Meter Exclusion Zone (as described in the Offshore Safety Division of the UK
Health & Safety Executive under section 22 of the Petroleum Act 1987) relates
to Upstream activities to be under the control of the Off-shore Construction or
Operations Manager. As such, they are treated as company premise.
Facilities or operations warranting an Exclusion Zone include:
 ADNOC Group’s offshore surface facilities, including permanent platforms,
offshore terminals, floating production vessels (FPSO, FSO), CALM Buoys,
etc.
 ADNOC Group’s offshore subsea components and subsea pipelines (or the
associated Right-of-Way (ROW)) only during the period in which work (e.g.,
surveying, construction, installation, or maintenance) in support of the
Company is being conducted on them
 The 500-Meter Exclusion Zone extends from the sea surface intersection of
the vertical projection of the facility or associated ROW.
Note: UAE Law # 17 of 2009 also stipulates requirements for establishing a
Safety Zone specified at a distance of 500 meter around a petroleum fixture or
installation or island measured from each point on its outer periphery in the
marine region in Abu Dhabi Emirate. This however focuses on authorising and
access control requirements for various activities within safety zones including
(a) entry of ships, hovering, (b) flying or landing in safety zones, (c) mooring or
tying boats or ships or anchoring in safety zones, and (d) fishing or hiking or
diving or swimming in safety zones
Fatality A death resulting from an injury or illness, regardless of the intervening time
between the injury and death.
Fatal Accident Rate The number of fatalities per 100,000,000 (100 million) work hours
[FAR]
First Aid Case [FAC] Any work related injury or illness that does not involve lost workdays, restricted
workdays or medical treatment but which receives First Aid Treatment. Refer to
Section 7.8.8 for list of specific treatments that constitutes FAC.

ADNOC Classification: Internal
HSE Management System
HSE Governance & Administration Standards
Incident Notification, Investigation & Reporting
TERMS DESCRIPTION
Flaring (a) Routine Flaring includes Continuous Production Flaring: Pilot & Purge/
Continuous Production Flaring is characterized by a routine, continuous gas
stream which is routed to a flare system because there is no economical gas
valorization route for a satellite field or for the routine venting of flash gas in
upstream production.
(b) Non-Routine Flaring involves operations which results in high emissions,
large scale emission of smoke, high noise levels or other unusual features;
any event where complaints could arise from regulatory bodies or other
authorities. Non Routine Flaring includes
 Initial Start-Up Flaring: which occurs during the commissioning and
initial start-up phases of a plant or process unit, when gas and liquids
are introduced into new facilities and equipment to test the production
of the products for sale (oil, gas, LPG, condensate, etc.).
 Planned Operational Flaring: which may occur for a number of
reasons including Maintenance and inspection; Modifications and
construction; Reservoir and well maintenance; Drilling and workovers
 Unplanned Operational Flaring: which may occur for a number of
reasons including Production dynamics; Mechanical failures in
machines; Instrument & valve failures; Failures due to erosion or
corrosion (internal and external); Electrical failures; Human factors: any
human action resulting in involuntary flaring (lack of preparation and
procedures, non-compliance with an existing procedure, etc.);
emergency shutdown with depressurization; Miscellaneous: causes that
have been identified but do not fit into any of the other categories;
Unexplained: any flared volume for which the exact cause is difficult to
identify (except problems with metering); Flaring to purge flare lines with
hydrocarbon gas, for safety purposes. Includes emergency shutdown
with depressurization.
Reference: Flaring Classification | IPIECA
Full Time Dedicated Also known in common use as “term charter”, “term contractor”, or “dedicated
Charter (Land or Air) charter/carrier”) is a land or air charter that is essentially dedicated for Company
use. Refer to Appendix 6 for examples.
Green Field Project A greenfield project is one which is not constrained by prior work. It is constructing
on unused land where there is no need to remodel or demolish an existing
structure
Group Company means any company within the ADNOC Group, other than ADNOC
HIPO High Potential (HIPO) Events are the incidents with a Potential Severity 4 or
above as per ADNOC Risk Matrix (Appendix 1).
HSE Flash HSE Flash ensures immediate and timely communication on the initial findings,
immediate actions (initial measures taken at location) and recommendations from
significant HSE occurrences.
Examples of HSE occurrences for issuing HSE Flash, include, but not limited to:
 Incidents (Severity 3 and above)
 High Potential Incidents (HIPO)
 Significant HSE Observations that provide learning across the
organization, such as:
o Serious HSE non-compliance
o Failure of Procedures / Processes (e.g. MoC, Risk Management etc.)

ADNOC Classification: Internal

TERMS DESCRIPTION
HSE Lesson Learned HSE Lessons Learned (HSELL) ensures communication of significant learning
(HSELL) from Investigation of HSE Occurrences (Incidents, HIPO, etc.).
HSELL are used for effective sharing of lessons learned within the ADNOC Group
to prevent similar recurrence.
HSE Observation A tool to observe and document hazards and violations related to unsafe
conditions, unsafe acts and ADNOC Life Saving Rules (LSR) within the work
environment. HSE Observations’ data analysis helps in identifying areas for
improving performance. Refer to “HSE Observation Report” (FM-GAST04-15).
Hurt Level In the Hurt Free Approach, the actual and potential consequence (Hurt Level) of
a safety or health work related event (personal injury / illness) in the work
environment is assessed according to six levels of injury severity, which
includes consideration of the duration of the effect. Refer to Section 7.13 for
details on Hurt Level determination and reporting requirements.
Incident An undesirable event or chain of events which cause, or could have caused
injury, illness, impact on company’s reputation, production, damage (loss) to the
assets and/or harm to environment, or third parties
Incident Owner Incident Owner is the site owner accountable for managing a specific work
location, equipment, activity / operation or person(s) involved in an incident.
Industrial hours Industrial hours defined as the time the employee is at official work for the
Employer. This includes the period during which the Employee is travelling from
their place of residence by the normal route to perform his or her duties and
returns to his to his or her place of residence by the usual route after completion
of duties.
Note: This is for any applicable Insurance & Compensation claims purposes in
line with ADNOC Group Human Capital & Administration requirements.
In-patient Hospitalization as a formal admission to the in-patient service of relevant local,
Hospitalization federal or international health authority’s licensed health care facility for care or
treatment.
IUCN International Union for Conservation of Nature
The IUCN Red List of Threatened Species, founded in 1964, is the world's most
comprehensive inventory of the global conservation status of biological species.
It uses a set of criteria to evaluate the extinction risk of thousands of species and
subspecies.
Licensed Health Care Health Care Professional (Consultant, Specialist, Medical Practitioners
Professional [LHCP] Physician, Occupational Medicine Specialist etc.) licensed by the relevant Govt.
Authority e.g. within UAE from Department of Health Abu Dhabi or Dubai Health
Authority. LHCP is expected to do any of the following:
 Independently diagnose medical conditions and independently treat or
prescribe for the diagnosed condition
 Engage in activities as qualified by training that include: hospitalization;
prescribing medications and other therapy; surgical debridement; utilizing
skin closing devices such as sutures, staples or surgical glue; complex
foreign body removal; ordering intravenous therapy, and prescribing or
providing physical therapy
 Diagnose and prescribe treatment for mental illness
 Diagnose musculoskeletal conditions and provide medical treatment,
including prescription medication, with or without physical therapy or
chiropractic manipulation.

Loss of Primary Loss of Primary Containment, an unplanned or uncontrolled release of any
Containment [LOPC] material from primary containment, including non-toxic and non-flammable
materials (e.g. steam, hot water, nitrogen, compressed CO , or compressed air).
2
Lost Time Injury / Any absence from work related Fatalities, Permanent Total Disabilities,
Illness [LTI] Permanent Partial Disabilities or Lost Workday Cases.
The number of LTIs is the sum of Fatalities, Permanent Total Disabilities,
Permanent Partial Disabilities and Lost Workday Cases.
Note: Restricted Work and Medical Treatment Cases are not included in LTIs.
Lost Time Injury / The number of Lost Time Injuries/Illness (LTIs) per 1,000,000 (million) hours
Illness Frequency worked.
[LTIF]
It is calculated by multiplying the number of LTIs by 1,000,000 and subsequently
dividing this by the exposure hours worked during the period.
Lost Time Injury / A measurement of the seriousness of injuries/illness and is the number of lost
Illness Severity (LTIS) workdays (estimated where necessary) per 1,000,000 (million) hours worked.
It is calculated by multiplying the number of Lost Workdays by 1,000,000 and
subsequently dividing this by the exposure hours worked during the period.
Lost Workday Case Any work-related injury or illness, other than a fatal injury, which results in a
[LWDC] person (being unfit for work on any day after the day of occurrence of the
occupational injury) temporarily unable to perform any regular job or restricted
work activity on a subsequent scheduled workday or shift. ‘Any day’ includes rest
days, weekend days, leave days, public holidays or days after ceasing
employment.
LWDC vs. LTI: A lost time injury is a reporting requirement and is a collection of
a number of different consequences that can occur following an incident, such as
a fatality, permanent disability etc. A single incident can give rise to several lost
workday cases, depending on the number of people injured as a result of that
incident.
Note: If a LWDC transforms into Fatality or Permanent Total Disability, it will no
longer be considered as LWDC. Incident Severity, Statistics and KPIs shall be
updated accordingly for severity of injury and number of lost workdays.
Lost Workdays The total number of scheduled workdays lost through occurrence of occupational
injuries / illnesses excluding the incident day.
The sum of total calendar days (consecutive or otherwise) after the day on which
the occupational injury or illness occurred, where person reported under LWDC
(above) was unfit for work and did not work.
Total number of Lost Workdays shall be limited to 180 scheduled working days.
It is not required to keep track of the number of days away from work if the injury
or illness resulted in more than 180 scheduled working days away from work.
Where absence from work extends beyond the year end, the actual or estimated
days unfit for work in the following year should be added to those for the reporting
year in computing the number of lost workdays.
In cases where employment is terminated after an Injury/illness occurred (other
than Fatality or Permanent Total Disability), this would include any previously
scheduled workdays lost that were planned prior to termination, or an estimate
must be made of the total number of workdays that would have been lost had the
employee not been terminated or to a maximum of 180 days.
If the employee decides to leave the company for any other reason unrelated to
the injury or illness, such as retirement, or to take another job, total number of

workdays lost should stop on the last day of notice period or upon fitness to return
to work is issued by the Occupational Health Physician whichever comes first.
In case of Death (Fatality) or Permanent Total Disability, No Lost Workday shall
be recorded.
Days unfit for work between a fatal incident and the date of death are not included.
Example - Three employees were severely injured and unfit for work after their
respective incidents. Employee A was unfit for 2 working days, a weekend and 2
further days. Employee B was unfit for 3 weeks, and Employee C was fit for work
the day after the injury but thereafter not fit for the three following days.
A was unfit for work for 2+2+2 days = 6 days
B was unfit for work for 3×7 days = 35 days
C was unfit for work for = 3 days
Number of days unfit for work = 30 days.
This example should be reported as 3 Lost Workday Cases with the total of 30
Lost Workdays.
May Indicates a permission
Medical Treatment Any work related injury or illness that involves neither lost workdays nor restricted
Case [MTC] workdays but which requires medical treatment beyond the first aid by physician
or other Licensed Health Care Professional.
Motor Vehicle Any mechanically powered vehicle used to transport people or property. This
includes motorcycles. Specifically excluded from the definition of motor vehicle
are vehicles operated on fixed rails and on-site vehicles that are not capable of
more than 10 mph (16 kph). If an incident occurs which involves the load, the
load on the vehicle is to be considered part of the vehicle.
Vehicles are split into four sub-categories:
 Heavy Vehicle (HV)
 Light Vehicle (LV)
 Mobile equipment
 Motorcycles
 A motor vehicle designed to transport/carry people
Motor Vehicle Crash Incident involving one or more vehicles, which could have caused harm to people,
(MVC) the environment, reputation, or any combination of these three elements.
Crash means work-related vehicle damage, personal injury or fatality due to a
vehicle related event, e.g. collision or rollover.
Near Miss An unplanned on uncontrolled event or chain of events that has not resulted in
recordable injury or physical damage or environmental damage but had the
potential to do so in other circumstances. Near-Miss definition includes all types of
events including Occupational Safety & Health, Environment and Process Safety.
Non-Accidental Death Non-accidental death is the death of a company or contractor employee due to
[NAD] suicide or a non-work related illness occurring in the work environment i.e. death
within employer’s premises.
Any case of death of a person either;
 Where there is no identifiable incident or trauma involved OR
 Is the result of an apparent suicide
Notification Notification as used in this Standard includes notifying the Reportable Incidents to
ADNOC within the timeframe and using the template prescribed in this Standard
Occupational Illness Any abnormal condition or disorder, or any fatality other than one resulting from an
occupational injury, caused by exposure to environmental factors associated with

ingestion of, or direct contact with the hazard, as well as exposure to physical and
psychological hazards. It will generally result from prolonged or repeated exposure.
Note: For the purpose of this Standard the terms illness and disease and

Occupational Injury An occupational injury is any injury which results from a work-related activity or
from an exposure involving a single incident in the work environment, such as
such as a cut, fracture, sprain, amputation, deafness from explosion, one-time
chemical exposure, back disorder from a slip/trip, insect or snake bite, etc.. For
the purpose of ADNOC HSE Standards the terms occupational and work related
can be used interchangeably
Permanent Partial Any work-related injury or illness which results in the complete loss or permanent
Disability [PPD] loss of use of any part(s) of the body or any permanent impairment of function or
parts of body, regardless of any pre-existing disability of the injured member of
impaired body function.
A PPD is not related to the ability of the injured person to do his/her normal work.
Thus, a PPD shall be classified if the worker has lost a finger, toe, arm, limb or
part thereof etc. but upon recovery was still able to perform normal work or any
other work that permits for the partial disability.
Note: Reference is made to the Federal Law of 1980 on Regulating Labour
Relations, Schedule 2 of which provides a detailed description of such injuries.
Permanent Total Any work-related injury or illness, which permanently incapacitates an employee
Disability [PTD] from doing any work & results in termination of employment or medical severance
Note: Reference is made to the Federal Law of 1980 on Regulating Labour
Relations, Schedule 2 which provides a detailed description of such injuries.
Primary Containment A tank, vessel, pipe, truck, rail car, or other equipment designed to keep a
material within it, typically for purposes of storage, separation, processing or
transfer of gases or liquids.
PPA ADNOC Petroleum Port Authority
Process Safety Event An unplanned or uncontrolled release of any material including non-toxic and
[PSE] non-flammable materials (e.g. steam, hot water, nitrogen, compressed CO , or
2
compressed air) from a process, or an undesired event or condition, that under
slightly different circumstances, could have resulted in a release of material.
Recordable Incident All Incidents are Recordable (Logged) in ADNOC Group incident recording
system, if it is in any way associated with the employees or activities of ADNOC
Group or a Reportable Contractor. The presence or absence of fault or
preventability is not relevant in determining whether or not an incident, injury or
illness is recordable.
Recordable Injury / Recordable Injuries / Illness requiring reporting in Total Recordable Injury Rate
Illness [TRIR] includes any work related injury or illness resulting in:
- Fatality or
- Permanent Total Disability or

Reportable Incident All work related incidents with Actual Severity 3 or above OR Potential Severity
4 or above involving ADNOC Group or Reportable Contractors’ employees
requiring notification to ADNOC within the timeframes prescribed in this
Standard.
Reportable Incidents include but not limited to the following. Refer to Section 7.7
& 7.14 for details.
- Fatality or NAD
- Permanent Total Disability or Permanent Partial Disability
- Inpatient Hospitalization of 24 hours or more for care or treatment
- Confirmed Cases of Serious Occupational Disease or Illness (Appendix 2)
- Lost Workdays Cases (more than 7 Days)
- HIPO with Potential Severity 4 or Higher
- Assets / Property Damage
- Environmental Incidents
- Loss of Well Control Incidents
- Tier 1 & Tier 2 Process Safety Event (LOPC) irrespective of Severity Level
- Fire incidents within Process Facilities / Area including Flame, Smoldering
and Smoke irrespective of Severity
- Motor Vehicle Crash (MVC) Incidents
- Incidents involving Reputational Impacts
Reportable Contractors operating under Contractual Mode 1 and 2 are defined as Reportable
Contractors Contractors for Incident Reporting requirements (see definitions of Contractual
Mode 1, 2 and Excluded Contractors)
Reporting to ADNOC Reporting as used in this Standard includes:
(a) Reporting the investigation findings for “Reportable Incidents” to ADNOC
within the timeframe and using templates (Forms) prescribed in this
Standard.
(b) Reporting the Recordable Incidents Statistics and KPIs in the HSE
Performance as required by ADNOC Standard on HSE Performance
Monitoring & Reporting (HSE-GA-ST08).
Restricted Work Restricted work activity occurs when the employee, because of the work-related
injury or illness, is unable to perform all or any part of his or her normal
assignment during all or any part of the normal workday or shift. The emphasis
is on the employee’s inability to perform normal job duties over a normal work
shift. “Normally assigned work” means any tasks that the employee performs or
is expected to perform as part of their job.
A case is not considered to be Restricted Work if the following three conditions
are met:
1. There is no medical treatment required;
2. The worker is fully capable of doing all tasks that had been scheduled to
be performed during the period;
3. There have been no explicit restrictions placed on the worker by LHCP
(treating doctor).

This allows implementing preventive measures to reduce the risk of aggravating
an otherwise minor injury.
Restricted Workday A work-related injury or illness other than a fatality or lost work day case which
Cases [RWDC] results in an individual being unable to perform all normally assigned work
functions during any scheduled work shift; or being assigned to another job on a
temporary or permanent basis after the day of the injury or illness. Less than
normal assigned work functions include:
 Performing all duties or normally assigned work functions but at less than
full-time schedule.
 Performing limited duties during normally assigned full-time schedule.
Work performed might be:
 an assignment to a temporary job
 part-time work at the regular job
 working full-time in the regular job but not performing all the usual duties
of the job.
Where no meaningful restricted work is being performed, the incident should be
reviewed for recording as a lost work day case (LWDC).
Rollover (MVC) Any crash where the vehicle has flipped onto any of its sides (90 degrees), top
and/or rolled 360 degrees via any axis.
Root Cause The initiating event that begins the chain of events that leads to an incident -
usually an active failure. Refer to Appendix 3 ADNOC List of Causes
Secondary An impermeable physical barrier specifically designed to prevent release of
containment materials into the environment that have breached primary containment
Similarly Exposed Worker population that are subjected to the same hazards in the work place and
Groups (SEGs) who experience a similar exposure pattern.
Severity Describes the extent of the actual and/or potential consequences of HSE,
reputation, financial and legal events. Refer to ADNOC Risk Matrix (Appendix 1)
Shareholder Means ADNOC or any third party shareholder of a Group Company. In the case
of an ADNOC wholly owned Group Company, then it means ADNOC only.
Shall / Must Indicates the mandatory requirement / action for compulsory compliance.
Should Indicates the recommended requirements
Sick Leave Sick leave is leave of absence from work due to incapacity to perform duties
resulting from any injury or illness occurrence, as prescribed by the treating LHCP
(Physician) in relevant local, federal or international health authority licensed
health care facility. Sick leave allows an employee to address their health &
safety needs to recover from illness, injury, or disability that prevents
performance of usual duties.
Site Means the operational site of Group Company where the operations of such
company take place.
Spot / Voyage Charter Also known as “common carrier” or “time charter” is a land or air charter that is
(Land or Air) non-exclusive to Company use, and other third parties can and do regularly use
the same carrier and carrier's equipment. Refer to Appendix 6 for examples.
Subject Matter Expert An individual with a deep understanding of a particular specialty, process,
(SME) function, technology, machine, material or type of equipment who can be called
to assist in investigations

Third Party Any individual other than an ADNOC Group Employee or a Reportable Contractor.
As these persons are not part of the Company-controlled workforce, they are not
considered for work place injury reporting; however, fatalities of such individuals
are recordable in some cases (see Section 7.8.1). Examples include customers,
visitors, vendors, delivery services with no contract, members of public.
In addition to Excluded Contractors, “Third Parties” would also include other
persons or employees of entities for which there is no direct contract to provide
service to the client.
Tier 1 - Process Safety A Tier 1 Process Safety Event (T-1 PSE) is a loss of primary containment (LOPC)
Event with the greatest consequence as defined by API RP 754. A T-1 PSE is an
[T-1 PSE] unplanned or uncontrolled release of any material, including non-toxic and
nonflammable materials (e.g. steam, hot water, nitrogen, compressed CO or
2
compressed air), from a process that results in one or more of the consequences
listed in API RP 754. Refer to Appendix 6 for examples
Note: refer API RP 754 for consequence details
Tier 2 - Process Safety A Tier 2 Process Safety Event (T-2 PSE) is a LOPC with lesser consequence. A
Event T-2 PSE is an unplanned or uncontrolled release of any material, including non-
[T-2 PSE] toxic and non-flammable materials (e.g. steam, hot water, nitrogen, compressed
CO , or compressed air), from a process that results in one or more of the
2
consequences listed in API RP 754 and is not reported as a Tier 1 PSE. Refer
to Appendix 6 for examples. Note: refer API RP 754 for consequence details
Tier 3 - Process Safety A Tier 3 PSE typically represents a challenge to the barrier system that
Event progressed along the path to harm, but is stopped short of a Tier 1 or Tier 2 PSE
[T-3 PSE] consequence. Indicators at this level provide an additional opportunity to identify
and correct weaknesses within the barrier system.
Note: refer to API RP 754 for details
TRT Tactical Response Team
Total Reportable Total reportable illnesses are the sum of all confirmed occupational illnesses (work-
Illnesses related), whether or not they resulted in deaths, permanent total disabilities,
permanent partial disabilities, lost workday cases, or restricted work cases
Total Reportable The total reportable illness frequency is the number of total reportable confirmed
Illness Frequency illnesses (work-related) per 1,000,000 [million] hours worked during the reporting
(TRIF) period. It is calculated using the following equation:
Total Reportable Illness Frequency = [Total Confirmed Reportable Illnesses
(work-related) x 1,000,000] / Total Man Hours Worked
Total Recordable The number of injuries (Fatalities + Permanent Total Disabilities + Permanent
Injury / Illness Rate Partial Disabilities + Lost Workday Cases + Restricted Work Cases + Medical
[TRIR] Treatment Cases) per 1,000,000 [million] hours worked. TRIR does not include
First Aid Cases.
Unsafe Act A violation of an accepted safe procedure or practice that could permit the
occurrence of an incident.
Unsafe Condition A hazardous physical condition or circumstance that could directly permit the
occurrence of an incident.
Worker The term “Worker” referred to Employees and/or Reportable Contractors in
contexts that apply equally to both.
Work Environment The work environment is composed of the employer’s premises and other
locations where employees or reportable contractors are engaged in work related
activities or they are present as a condition of their employment. The work

environment includes not only physical location, but also the equipment or
material used by the employee during the course of his or her work.
As such, the Work Environment includes:
 Company Premises
 Off Company premise locations at which work-related activity is conducted
 Locations where a worker is present as a condition of employment
 Locations and transportation associated with work-related travel
Working Hours The total number of hours of employment (work performed) including overtime
(Exposure Hours) and training but excluding leave, sickness and other absences.
Exposure to health hazards & to the possibility of contracting an occupational
illness must be measured by the total number of working hours of:
 All employees of ADNOC Group;
 All employees of reportable contractors whilst engaged on work for the
ADNOC Group under contractual mode 1 and 2.
Working hours must be calculated from time sheets or other records. When this
is not possible these may be estimated by multiplying the total number of days
worked during the reporting period by the number of hours worked per day.
Well Control Events Failure of one or more well barriers such as reduction in drilling fluid head leading
to influx from the wellbore is failure of well barrier; this would require closure of

6. ROLES AND RESPONSIBILITIES
6.1. ADNOC GROUP
Senior Management (VPs and above) at ADNOC Directorates & Functions in HQ, Group Companies
and Affiliates is accountable for ensuring implementation of this Standard. Key expectations include:
 Providing adequate resources (personnel and financial) to ensure effective and timely recording /
reporting, classification, escalation, investigation, closeout and communication of incidents.
 Ensure reporting of all High Severity Incidents (actual severity 3 or above) and HIPO (potential
severity 4 or above) to ADNOC within the established timeframe
 Assigning Incident Owner
 Approving Terms of Reference (ToR) (Ref Section 6.10)
 Appointing Investigation Team and conducting appropriate level of investigation to identify the root
causes of the incident.
 Tracking and reviewing of actions of the incident investigation till its completion
 Ensuring competent and trained personnel available for performing investigations
 Ensure compliance with the requirements of incident reporting to concerned Federal and Local
Authorities including Federal Authority for Nuclear Regulations (FANR).
Refer to Section 7.21 for additional other responsibilities.
6.2. ADNOC CONTROL ROOM
ADNOC Control Room Supervisor must:
 Inform relevant Duty Manager(s) from the concerned Directorate(s) about Initial Incident Notification.
 Contact the relevant Duty Manager(s) from the concerned Directorate(s) and provide them with
Preliminary Incident Notification Report (FM-GAST04-01).
6.3. ADNOC DUTY MANAGERS
ADNOC Duty Managers must:
 Follow up the incident in accordance with their procedures and notify ADNOC senior management
(VPs and above) accordingly. Based on the severity of the incident, the Senior Management of each
respective Directorate will then inform the Chief Executive Officer (CEO) of ADNOC.
Note: Any response or communication by ADNOC to outside parties will be a decision by ADNOC
Senior Management. Such response will typically involve Public Relations and the responsible Line
Directorate.
6.4. ADNOC GROUP HSE
The role of ADNOC Group HSE in responding to notified / reported incidents includes but not limited to:
 Briefing the ADNOC Chief Executive Officer (CEO) about the incident
 Maintaining a track of the reported incident with a view to incorporate these in corporate performance
statistics.
 Providing technical advice on HSE matters.
 Ensuring that all incidents across ADNOC Group are defined, classified and recorded as per
requirements of this Standard.
 SVP ADNOC Group HSE Function is the final authority for reclassification of Incidents in ADNOC.
The decision made by SVP Group HSE Function on incidents’ reclassification shall be final and not

 Verifying compliance to this Standard by receiving and reviewing the Incident Notification,
Investigation ToR and Reports for all reportable incidents.
 Providing assistance to the Investigation Process as deemed necessary;
 Lead or participate in incident investigations.
 Review the investigation reports for Reportable Incidents to verify compliance with this Standard.

 Coordinating the dissemination of HSE Flash and Lessons Learned across ADNOC Group.

6.5. ADNOC GROUP COMPANY CORPORATE HSE
The key roles of Group Company HSE related to implementation of this Standards includes:
 Ensuring the requirements of this Standard are clearly communicated and understood by the end
users
 Ensure compliance with the Incident Management requirements including:
- Incident notification, classification, recording and reporting
- Incident Severity (Actual and Potential) correctly assigned by the Incident Owner.
- Classify incidents, including injury / illness in consultation with OH SME (as applicable)
- Incident Notification to ADNOC Group HSE within required timeframe.
- Review Investigation Terms of Reference
 ADNOC Group Company / Functions / Affiliate’s VP Corporate HSE or equivalent is responsible and
accountable for “Incident Classification” as per requirements of this Standard. This includes
establishing work relatedness, assigning severity and any change in classification, if required. The
VP Corporate HSE or equivalent shall maintain duly approved, auditable record of Incident
Classification establishing justification with all supporting documents. This includes incidents
involving Reportable Contractors as well as ADNOC Group Company working as a Reportable
Contractor.
 Support Incident Investigation Process
- Participate in Investigation of Incidents with Severity 3 or above.
- Review and endorse Incidents Investigation Reports
- Ensure Investigation Reports completed and submitted within required timeframe.
- Review and endorse HSE Flash and HSE Lessons Learned.
- Managing “conflicting contemporaneous medical recommendations” as per requirements of
Section 7.10.
- Monitor progress on Corrective Actions as per assigned targets
Refer to Section 7.21 for additional other responsibilities

6.6. ADNOC DIRECTORATES
The key roles related to implementation of this Standards includes:
 Ensuring the requirements of this Standard are clearly communicated and understood by the end
users.
 Ensure compliance with the Incident Management requirements including:
- Incident Severity (Actual and Potential) correctly assigned by the Incident Owner.
- Incident Notification to ADNOC Group HSE within required timeframe.
- Issuance of Investigation Terms of Reference.
- Ensure Investigation Reports completed and submitted within required timeframe.
- Monitor progress on Corrective Actions as per assigned targets
6.7. ADNOC GROUP MEDICAL SERVICES DIVISION
The role of ADNOC Group Medical Services Division includes:
 Nominating a member for investigation as and if requested by the Incident / NAD Owner and /or
ADNOC Group HSE Function.
 Provide necessary support for investigating occupational health incidents and Non Accidental Death
(NAD).
 Ensure investigation team leader is provided access to employees’ medical records as required for
the investigation process.
6.8. ADNOC GROUP COMPANY – HUMAN CAPITAL & ADMINISTRATION
 Maintain and Review employees’ medical records (medical check-up, health surveillance and
sickness absence, insurance claims etc.) and furnish relevant information to ADNOC Group OH
SMEs as and when required for OH injury / illness investigation.
 Communicates to concerned HSE team members and management, the findings of health
surveillance or investigation reports of suspected and/or confirmed Occupational disease case.
 Provides required support to the Occupational disease investigation team formed by the Group
Company’s Management for the purpose.
6.9. ADNOC GROUP COMPANY – OCCUPATIONAL HEALTH (OH) SME / OH
SPECIALIST / INDUSTRIAL HYGIENE (IH) ENGINEER OR EQUIVALENT
 Review employees’ medical records (medical check-up, health surveillance and sickness absence,
insurance claims etc.) and identifies suspected cases of occupational disease, if any, for establishing
work relatedness, recordability, further investigation and confirmation.
 Identifies a specific employee or a SEG (Select Employee Group) for health surveillance based on
the results of exposure monitoring and Occupational Health Risk Assessment (OHRA).
 Implement Injury/Illness classification process as per requirements of this Standard
 Conducts specific employee exposure monitoring or workplace assessment as recommended by
medical team for investigation of a suspected Occupational disease case.
 Maintains records of exposure monitoring, findings from health surveillance and Occupational
disease case investigation reports.
 Communicates Occupational disease case to ADNOC HSE as per requirement and through standard
reporting mechanism.

6.10. INCIDENT OWNER
 Incident Owner is accountable for managing a specific work location, equipment, activity / operation
or person(s) involved in an incident. The level of incident ownership shall be determined based on
the severity of the incident (Refer to Section 7.15).
Refer to Appendix 7 for establishing incident ownership for incidents involving multiple Group
Companies.
 Incident owners shall be responsible for:
- Distributing the TOR to the Investigation Team
- Distributing final investigation report and the recommendations to action owners and relevant
stakeholders.
- Reviewing investigation recommendations for any requirement to activate Consequence
Assessment Committee (CAC).
- Preparing HSE Flash and HSE Lessons Learned from Incidents and ensuring that the
Lesson Learned are shared as per requirements of this Standard (Refer to Section 7.21).
- Conducting Roadshows for Severity 5 and 6 Incidents to share lessons learned and create
awareness to avoid recurrence
- Preparing Action Plan to address the investigation’s recommendations by assigning specific
actions and due dates to Action Parties for implementation.
- Ensuring that corrective actions are implemented and closed out. Providing regular update
on status of action items to Group HSE as per the agreed frequency.
Refer to Section 7.17.4 for additional responsibilities related to presentation to ADNOC ELT and
Management.
6.11. TERMS OF REFERENCE (TOR) SIGNATORY
 The level of TOR signatory shall be determined based on the severity of the incident (Refer to Section
7.15). TOR Signatory shall be responsible for:
- Approving and Issuing the Terms of Reference
- Nominating the team for conducting the incident investigation.
- Ensuring that the Investigation Team is provided with applicable tools and equipment needed
for investigation.
6.12. INVESTIGATION TEAM
 Investigate the circumstances leading up to the incident and establish sequence of events.
 Investigate the response to the incident, including emergency actions taken.
 Analyze the information gathered, identify the immediate/root causes, review & verify effectiveness
of existing controls and identify missing controls (if any).
 Interview all involved/witnesses as per investigation process.
 Make recommendations to avoid recurrence of similar incidents and to improve the incident response
as appropriate.
 Prepare Preliminary and Final Investigation Reports.
Refer to Section 7.15 for Investigation Team’s expectations

 ADNOC Group Employees and Reportable Contractors are responsible for:
- Reporting Incidents and Near-Misses in a timely manner.
- Proactively reporting unsafe acts / behaviour and /or unsafe conditions to prevent incidents.
- Cooperate in Incident Investigation, as and when required.
- Contractors shall align their Incident Reporting and Investigation process with the
requirements of this Standard.

7. REQUIREMENTS
7.1. INCIDENT NOTIFICATION, INVESTIGATION AND REPORTING FRAMEWORK
All incidents must be logged, notified, investigated and reported as required by Group Companies in
line with the requirements of this Standard. Each Group Company shall record, manage and report
incidents of all of their respective employees and contractors regardless of the incident location or
cause. Reporting is to reflect work-related injuries/illnesses incidents affecting both Employees and
Reportable Contractors (and in the case of fatalities, affected Third Parties), whether these occur on
Company premises or in other work environments/situations.
The terms recordable (injuries), reportable (incidents) and work relatedness used in this standard
doesn’t exempt the incident owner from investigating ALL incidents occurring in the work environment.
The objective of the investigation for non-work related incidents is to identify causes and contributors
that help improve the work environment.
When an incident is observed, initial response must be provided to secure the incident location and
prevent further escalation of the situation. This response must be in line with the Emergency / Facility
Response Plan (ERP/ FRP) of the facility or location where the incident has occurred. The potential
severity shall also be evaluated in order to determine the level of ownership and investigation required.
An Investigation team shall be nominated to carry out investigation and identify the causes with
recommendations to address the causes. For the investigation purpose, the following actions must be
implemented only if they don’t aggravate the situation or don’t interfere with emergency response actions:
 Gather preliminary information
 Preserve and secure any evidence
 Record and document site conditions (photographs, sketches etc.)
 Identify possible cause / effect
As part of remedial action follow-up process, recommendations from the incident investigation must be
developed into actions for implementation. Learnings from incidents shall be shared across the ADNOC
Group with necessary actions plans implemented by all GCs, as applicable.
The framework of the incident management process composed of incident notification, investigation,
reporting, corrective actions’ follow-up and learning from incidents. The process flow from incident
notification to learning from incidents is outlined in Figure 7.1.1 and the RACI Matrix in Table 7.1.1.
Appendix 9 provides Incidents’ Notification and Reporting Templates1 as below:

7.2. ESTABLISHING WORK RELATEDNESS
ADNOC incident recording and reporting is intended to capture cases that are caused by conditions or
exposures arising in the work environment. All incidents occurring within the work environment shall
however considered work-related, unless demonstrated otherwise based on detailed investigation. The
presence or absence of fault or preventability is not relevant in determining whether or not an injury or
illness is recordable (establishing work relatedness).
Work relatedness is established when an incident including the injury or illness occurring from, during
and by reason of employee practice of his/her job or from exposure involving single event (or multiple
events linked close together in time) in the Work Environment. The decision-making process for
establishing work relatedness is illustrated in Figure 7.2.1. Exceptions to work relatedness are detailed
in Section 7.2.1. Refer to Appendix 6 for examples on establishing work relatedness.
The Work Environment (Section 7.3) is composed of:
 The employer’s premises or
 Off company where employees or reportable contractors are engaged in work-related activities or
 Locations where employees or reportable contractors are present for purposes relating to their
employment or
 Locations and transportation associated with work related travel
However, even in the Work Environment, certain situations are excluded for reporting as work-related
injuries (Section 7.2.1).
The key requirements for determining work relatedness are:
 Injuries and illnesses that result from events or exposures occurring in the Work Environment
are considered Work-Related unless one or more of the listed Work Relatedness Exceptions
(Section 7.2.1) specifically apply.
 An injury or illness shall be Work-Related if an event or exposure in the Work Environment (i.e.,
any location where one or more Workers are either working or present as a condition of
employment, Section 7.3.3):
- Caused or contributed to the resulting condition OR
- Significantly aggravated a Pre-Existing Injury or Illness.
If it is not obvious whether an event or exposure occurred in the Work Environment, an evaluation of
the Worker's work duties and environment must occur to make a determination whether it is “more
likely than not” that a work-related event or exposure caused, contributed to, or significantly
aggravated the injury or illness. Section 7.5 provides the process to be used to determine work-
relatedness of an injury or illness in cases where the relation to the Work Environment is unclear.
Establishing the “work relatedness” of an incident, involves determining (Figure 7.2.1):
 Did incident occur in the Work Environment? (Section 7.3)
- Individual on Company Premises? (Section 7.3.1)
- Individual working elsewhere? (Section 7.3.2)
- Individual required to be present? (Section 7.3.3)
- Individual commuting or on work-related travel? (Section 7.4)
 Is incident subject to Work-relatedness Exceptions? (Section 7.2.1)
 Did an event or exposure in Work Environment cause, contribute to, or significantly aggravate
the injury or illness?
Stop / Suspended Work Activity: Once the ADNOC Group Company notifies the Contractor to stop
/ suspend the work activity. Any incident occurring thereafter within work environment should not be
considered work related. In this case, documented communication by Group Company shall confirm
the suspension / stoppage of work activity prior to the incident. Examples includes but not limited to
Standard No.: HSE-GA-ST04 Version No.: 1 Effective date: Dec., 2019 Page 31 of 184

ADNOC Classification: Internal
HSE Management System
HSE Governance & Administration Standards
Incident Notification, Investigation & Reporting
incidents involving heavy machinery (e.g. mobile crane, diggers, pile drivers, winching equipment),
marine vessels, etc. while non-operating and either on standby / suspended or finished work status
within the work environment. The incident shall however, still be reported and investigated as per the
requirements of this Standard to avoid such recurrences in futures.
All non-work related injuries and illnesses e.g. incidents occurring while commuting from home to work
or vice versa should be reported to the relevant responsible ADNOC Functions (Human Capital &
Administration, Group Medical Services Division, Group Risk Management Division, etc.) for Industrial
and Non-Industrial Classification required for insurance and compensation purposes (as applicable).

7.2.1. Work Relatedness Exceptions
An injury or illness occurring in the work environment that falls under one of the following exceptions is
not work-related. Other exceptions may be identified by the investigation team depending upon the
circumstances and nature of activity employee was involved in. Incident Owners should however
decide on investigating these incidents considering the associated risk level. Refer to Appendix 5 for
explanation to below listed exceptions:
1. At the time of the injury or illness, the injured person was present in the work environment as a
member of the general public rather than as an employee performing his/her normal work
duties.
2. The injury or illness involves signs or symptoms that are observed at work but result solely from
a non-work-related event or exposure that occurs outside the work environment. The injury or
illness was a pre-existing condition and the work activity / environment at the time of the incident
did not aggravate the pre-existing injury, or illness.
3. The injury or illness results solely from voluntary participation in a wellness, health, fitness
program or recreational activity (e.g. blood donation, flu vaccination, exercise class, sports
activity).
4. The injury or illness is solely the result of an employee eating, drinking, or preparing food or
drink for personal consumption (whether bought on the employer's premises or brought in).
Note: If the employee is made ill by ingesting food contaminated by workplace contaminants
(such as lead), or gets food poisoning from food supplied by the employer, the case would be
considered work-related. Refer to Section 7.8.4 “Occupational Illnesses”
5. The injury or illness is solely the result of an employee performing personal tasks (unrelated to
their employment) in the work environment, outside of the employee’s assigned working hours.
6. The injury or illness is solely the result of personal grooming or self-medication for a non-work
related condition, or is intentionally self-inflicted.
7. The injury or illness is caused by a motor vehicle crash occurring in the Work Environment (e.g.,
a Company parking lot or access road) while the employee is commuting to or from work.
8. The illness is a common cold or influenza (flu).
Note: In case of other infectious diseases such as malaria, dengue fever, tuberculosis,
brucellosis, Hepatitis etc. listed in Appendix 2 “Serious Occupational Diseases / Illnesses”, GC
HSE must evaluate such occurrences for work relationship. These diseases are considered
work-related if the employee is infected at work. Refer to ADNOC Health Screening &
Surveillance Standard (HSE-OH-ST05)
9. The illness is a mental illness. Mental illness will not be considered work-related unless the
employee voluntarily provides the employer with an opinion from a physician or other licensed
health care professional with appropriate training and experience (psychiatrist, psychologist,
psychiatric nurse practitioner, etc.) stating that the employee has a mental illness that is work-
related. Refer to Section 7.2.3.
Additionally, refer to below sections for exceptions to specific incidents / events:
(i) Section 7.3.4 & 7.3.5 for Incidents at Company’s / Contractors Controlled Accommodation
(ii) Section 7.4.1 for Commuting Incidents Work Relatedness
(iii) Section 7.4.5 for Reporting incidents during work related travel
(iv) Section 7.4.7 for Reporting Motor Vehicle Crash
(v) Section 7.7.4 for Reporting Contagious Diseases
(vi) Section 7.18 for Reporting NAD Events
Standard No.: HSE-GA-ST04 Version No.: 1 Effective date: Dec., 2019 Page 33 of 184

ADNOC Classification: Internal
HSE Management System
HSE Governance & Administration Standards
Incident Notification, Investigation & Reporting
7.2.2. Work-Relatedness of Cases Involving Delayed Notification
Workers are required to report all work-related injuries and illnesses, regardless of the severity, to their
Supervisor as soon as the injury occurs or when injury/illness symptoms are recognized. However, in
some cases, reporting is delayed. While delay in reporting can reduce the ability to identify a clear link
of the injury/illness to the Work Environment, the delayed reporting should not be used as a determinant
for excluding a case for reporting. Such cases often require additional investigation.
7.2.3. Work-Relatedness of Cases Involving Mental Illness
Mental illness is not Work-Related unless it is confirmed through voluntary submission by the Worker
of an opinion from an appropriately qualified LHCP that states that the illness is work-related (per
Exception #9 in the Work-relatedness Exceptions in Section 7.2.1).
GC HSE OH SME is required to review cases involving mental illness. This review typically includes
obtaining the Worker’s confidential medical records (information that cannot be released to
management for reasons of medical confidentiality) and interviewing the Worker (and other involved
parties as applicable).
Refer to Appendix 6 for examples on cases involving Mental Illness
7.2.4. Work-Relatedness of Miscellaneous Cases
Work Relatedness & Reportability of Cases [in Work Environment] resulting due to Violence,
Altercation (a heated or angry dispute; noisy argument or controversy), Anger, Act of Nature (Lightning
Strike), etc. and road incidents where Traffic Police establish fault by other party. Refer to Appendix 6
Section 9 for examples of these events.
All such cases are considered work-related, unless demonstrated otherwise based on investigation.
Incident to follow the full cycle of Notification, Recording, Investigation and Reporting.
Reporting in HSE Performance shall accordingly be evaluated based on the investigation findings
(establishing work-relatedness).
